=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and two integers, x and k. You can perform the following operation any number of times (including zero):

Increase or decrease any element of nums by 1.

Return the minimum number of operations needed to have at least k non-overlapping subarrays of size exactly x in nums, where all elements within each subarray are equal.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [5,-2,1,3,7,3,6,4,-1], x = 3, k = 2
Output: 8
Explanation:

Use 3 operations to add 3 to nums[1] and use 2 operations to subtract 2 from nums[3]. The resulting array is [5, 1, 1, 1, 7, 3, 6, 4, -1].
Use 1 operation to add 1 to nums[5] and use 2 operations to subtract 2 from nums[6]. The resulting array is [5, 1, 1, 1, 7, 4, 4, 4, -1].
Now, all elements within each subarray [1, 1, 1] (from indices 1 to 3) and [4, 4, 4] (from indices 5 to 7) are equal. Since 8 total operations were used, 8 is the output.


Example 2:

Input: nums = [9,-2,-2,-2,1,5], x = 2, k = 2
Output: 3
Explanation:

Use 3 operations to subtract 3 from nums[4]. The resulting array is [9, -2, -2, -2, -2, 5].
Now, all elements within each subarray [-2, -2] (from indices 1 to 2) and [-2, -2] (from indices 3 to 4) are equal. Since 3 operations were used, 3 is the output.


 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 10^5
-10^6 <= nums[i] <= 10^6
2 <= x <= nums.length
1 <= k <= 15
2 <= k * x <= nums.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minOperations(self, nums: List[int], x: int, k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Cost for a subarray = min over target value v of sum_{i in interval} |nums[i]-v| = median cost. Need choose k disjoint intervals length x minimizing sum costs. Need output min operations.

Extra intervals add nonnegative cost, so optimum at least k can be achieved by exactly k (drop extra reduces cost), so exactly k.

Now, let's think about Fenwick implementation details and performance.

Fenwick class:
class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self, size):
        self.n = size
        self.bit = [0]*(size+1)
    def add(self, idx, delta):
        n=self.n; bit=self.bit
        while idx<=n:
            bit[idx]+=delta
            idx += idx & -idx
    def sum(self, idx):
        s=0; bit=self.bit
        while idx>0:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return s
    def kth(self, target): # smallest idx with prefix >= target
        idx=0
        bitmask=1 << (self.n.bit_length()-1) maybe highest power of two <= n
        while bitmask:
            nxt = idx + bitmask
            if nxt <= self.n and self.bit[nxt] < target:
                idx = nxt
                target -= self.bit[nxt]
            bitmask >>=1
        return idx+1

Need two BITs count and sum. Could combine into one class with two arrays? For speed, maybe implement functions with local arrays.

If value=1e6 => idx=2,000,001. size = max_val - min_val + 1 = 2,000,001. offset = 1 - min_val. idx = value + offset. Because value + (1 - min_val) = value - min_val +1. Good.

Fenwick sum BIT stores actual value (not offset) for sum. For value negative, sum can be negative.

Fenwick functions with array:
def add(bit, idx, delta):
    n = M
    while idx <= n:
        bit[idx] += delta
        idx += idx & -idx

For sum:
def prefix(bit, idx):
    s = 0
    while idx:
        s += bit[idx]
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return s

For kth:
def kth(target):
    idx=0
    bitmask = top_bit
    while bitmask:
        nxt = idx + bitmask
        if nxt <= M and bit_cnt[nxt] < target:
            idx = nxt
            target -= bit_cnt[nxt]
        bitmask >>= 1
    return idx + 1

Need note: In kth, `bit_cnt[nxt]` is Fenwick tree value, not prefix. Standard algorithm works because bit array stores Fenwick sums. Need ensure `idx` starts 0 and bitmask highest power of two <= M. Good.

Pseudo:

from typing import List
from array import array

class Solution:
    def minOperations(self, nums: List[int], x: int, k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        m = n - x + 1
        
        min_val = min(nums)
        max_val = max(nums)
        size = max_val - min_val + 1
        offset = 1 - min_val
        
        cnt_bit = array('i', [0]) * (size + 1)
        sum_bit = array('q', [0]) * (size + 1)
        
        def add(bit, idx, delta):
            while idx <= size:
                bit[idx] += delta
                idx += idx & -idx
        
        def prefix(bit, idx):
            s = 0
            while idx > 0:
                s += bit[idx]
                idx -= idx & -idx
            return s
        
        top = 1 << (size.bit_length() - 1)
        def kth(target):
            idx = 0
            bitmask = top
            while bitmask:
                nxt = idx + bitmask
                if nxt <= size and cnt_bit[nxt] < target:
                    idx = nxt
                    target -= cnt_bit[nxt]
                bitmask >>= 1
            return idx + 1
        
        total_sum = 0
        for val in nums[:x]:
            idx = val + offset
            add(cnt_bit, idx, 1)
            add(sum_bit, idx, val)
            total_sum += val
        
        costs = [0] * m
        p = (x + 1) // 2
        for start in range(m):
            med_idx = kth(p)
            cnt_less = prefix(cnt_bit, med_idx - 1)
            sum_less = prefix(sum_bit, med_idx - 1)
            cnt_le = prefix(cnt_bit, med_idx)
            sum_le = prefix(sum_bit, med_idx)
            med_val = med_idx - offset
            cost = med_val * cnt_less - sum_less + (total_sum - sum_le) - med_val * (x - cnt_le)
            costs[start] = cost
            
            if start + x < n:
                old = nums[start]
                old_idx = old + offset
                add(cnt_bit, old_idx, -1)
                add(sum_bit, old_idx, -old)
                total_sum -= old
                
                new = nums[start + x]
                new_idx = new + offset
                add(cnt_bit, new_idx, 1)
                add(sum_bit, new_idx, new)
                total_sum += new
        
        # free? 
        del cnt_bit, sum_bit
        
        INF = 10**30
        dp_prev = [0] * m
        for j in range(1, k + 1):
            dp_cur = [INF] * m
            for i in range(m):
                if i > 0:
                    dp_cur[i] = dp_cur[i-1]
                if j == 1:
                    take = costs[i]
                elif i >= x:
                    take = dp_prev[i - x] + costs[i]
                else:
                    take = INF
                if take < dp_cur[i]:
                    dp_cur[i] = take
            dp_prev = dp_cur
        return dp_prev[m-1]

Need check if `dp_prev` for j=1 is initialized as zeros. For j=1, we don't use dp_prev. For j=2, dp_prev is dp1. Good.

But wait for j=1, dp_cur[i] = min(dp_cur[i-1], costs[i]). If costs[i] can be negative? No cost nonnegative. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can reduce branch by handling j=1 separately:
dp_prev = [0]*m
for j in range(1,k+1):
    dp_cur = [INF]*m
    if j == 1:
        best = INF
        for i,c in enumerate(costs):
            if c < best: best = c
            dp_cur[i] = best
    else:
        for i in range(m):
            ...
But current branch fine. However, branch `if j == 1` inside inner loop repeated k*m ~1.5e6, negligible. But we can optimize for clarity:
dp_prev = [0] * m
for j in range(1, k + 1):
    dp_cur = [INF] * m
    for i in range(m):
        if i:
            dp_cur[i] = dp_cur[i-1]
        if j == 1:
            take = costs[i]
        elif i >= x:
            take = dp_prev[i - x] + costs[i]
        else:
            take = INF
        if take < dp_cur[i]:
            dp_cur[i] = take
    dp_prev = dp_cur
return dp_prev[-1]

This is concise. Could there be an issue for j=1 and i=0: dp_cur[0]=INF, take=costs[0], update. For i>0, dp_cur[i]=dp_cur[i-1] (min previous), take=costs[i], update. Good.

Let's derive cost formula one more time to avoid sign error:
cost = sum_{v<M} (M-v) + sum_{v>M} (v-M)
= M*L - S_L + (S_G - M*G)
S_G = total - S_L - M*C_eq
G = x - L - C_eq
=> M*L - S_L + total - S_L - M*C_eq - M*x + M*L + M*C_eq
= 2M*L - 2S_L + total - M*x.
Yes. If M negative, e.g., M=-5, L=0, total=-15, x=3 => cost = -15 - (-5*3)= -15+15=0. Good. If L>0, okay.

Let's write functions:
def add(bit, idx, delta):
    while idx <= size:
        bit[idx] += delta
        idx += idx & -idx

def prefix(bit, idx):
    s = 0
    while idx > 0:
        s += bit[idx]
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return s

top_bit = 1 << (size.bit_length() - 1)
def kth(target):
    idx = 0
    bitmask = top_bit
    while bitmask:
        nxt = idx + bitmask
        if nxt <= size and cnt_bit[nxt] < target:
            idx = nxt
            target -= cnt_bit[nxt]
        bitmask >>= 1
    return idx + 1

This is fine.

=== ANSWER ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minOperations(self, nums: List[int], x: int, k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        m = n - x + 1

        # Fenwick trees over compressed value range.
        min_val = min(nums)
        max_val = max(nums)
        size = max_val - min_val + 1
        offset = 1 - min_val

        cnt_bit = [0] * (size + 1)
        sum_bit = [0] * (size + 1)

        def add(bit, idx, delta):
            while idx <= size:
                bit[idx] += delta
                idx += idx & -idx

        def prefix(bit, idx):
            s = 0
            while idx > 0:
                s += bit[idx]
                idx -= idx & -idx
            return s

        top_bit = 1 << (size.bit_length() - 1)

        def kth(target):
            idx = 0
            bitmask = top_bit
            while bitmask:
                nxt = idx + bitmask
                if nxt <= size and cnt_bit[nxt] < target:
                    idx = nxt
                    target -= cnt_bit[nxt]
                bitmask >>= 1
            return idx + 1

        total_sum = 0
        for i in range(x):
            val = nums[i]
            idx = val + offset
            add(cnt_bit, idx, 1)
            add(sum_bit, idx, val)
            total_sum += val

        costs = [0] * m
        median_pos = (x + 1) // 2

        for start in range(m):
            med_idx = kth(median_pos)
            med_val = med_idx - offset

            cnt_less = prefix(cnt_bit, med_idx - 1)
            sum_less = prefix(sum_bit, med_idx - 1)

            # Cost to make all elements in this window equal to the median.
            cost = 2 * med_val * cnt_less - 2 * sum_less + total_sum - med_val * x
            costs[start] = cost

            if start + x < n:
                old = nums[start]
                old_idx = old + offset
                add(cnt_bit, old_idx, -1)
                add(sum_bit, old_idx, -old)
                total_sum -= old

                new = nums[start + x]
                new_idx = new + offset
                add(cnt_bit, new_idx, 1)
                add(sum_bit, new_idx, new)
                total_sum += new

        # DP: choose exactly k non-overlapping windows.
        INF = 10 ** 30
        dp_prev = [0] * m

        for j in range(1, k + 1):
            dp_cur = [INF] * m
            for i in range(m):
                if i > 0:
                    dp_cur[i] = dp_cur[i - 1]

                if j == 1:
                    take = costs[i]
                elif i >= x:
                    take = dp_prev[i - x] + costs[i]
                else:
                    take = INF

                if take < dp_cur[i]:
                    dp_cur[i] = take

            dp_prev = dp_cur

        return dp_prev[-1]
```
